The body therefore lands with a minimal `/quit`-only\n37\t   inputs sequence and leans on `Expect.settings_json` rather than\n38\t   `Expect.file`.\n39\t\n40\tThe body must:\n41\t\n42\t- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the\n43\t  `deploy_hooks_to` call invoked by the setup wrapper is the\n44\t  effective \"first deploy\" of the OQ-2 contract;\n45\t- assert that the merged `settings.json` carries hook registrations\n46\t  for **every** hook event in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` —\n47\t  the `has_registration(<all hook events>, <all matchers>)` predicate\n48\t  from OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12, mapped to existing\n49\t  `Expect.settings_json` primitive checks (mapping documented in §3\n50\t  and §8.1 below);\n51\t- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n52\t  contract — sibling to E3-E11;\n53\t- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — `install_hooks` is a\n54\t  pure stdlib operation; no MCP, no network.\n55\t\n56\tThe \"twice in a row\" element of the OQ-2 input shape (and its paired\n57\t`digest unchanged` expect) cannot be expressed in the current\n58\tdeclarative DSL (the schema has no `callback:` field — see §3\n59\t\"Schema-expressibility constraint\" below). The strict declarative form\n60\tis deferred to a follow-up task per the established T05.07..T05.16\n61\tprecedent: land the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\n62\tdeclarative proxies, document the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict\n63\tform on the YAML callback escape hatch / a future\n64\t`adapter:` schema extension.\n65\t\n66\t## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hook_adapter.py` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n67\t\n68\t`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py:183-256` `deploy_hooks_to`:\n69\t\n70\t```python\n71\tdef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None:\n72\t    \"\"\"Deploy SuperClaude hooks into the per-eval HOME at ``home_path``.\n73\t\n74\t    Two filesystem effects, in this order:\n75\t    1. install_hooks() copies hook scripts to <home>/.claude/hooks/\n76\t       and merges hook registrations into <home>/.claude/settings.json.\n77\t    2. Source src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json is copied verbatim to\n78\t       <home>/.claude/hooks.json (byte-identical via shutil.copy2).\n79\t\n80\t    Idempotent: re-invocation on the same home_path produces identical\n81\t    filesystem state.\n82\t    \"\"\"\n83\t```\n84\t\n85\tThe idempotency contract of interest (OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12):\n86\t\n87\t| Observable | Purpose |\n88\t|---|---|\n89\t| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the first deploy | proves the install_hooks merge pipeline produced the expected target shape |\n90\t| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the second deploy | proves the second deploy did NOT remove/break the registrations |\n91\t| The settings.json digest is unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate matcher entries) | proves the second deploy is a true no-op — the bug class from PR #49 was exactly this: matcher exists but a second deploy duplicated it |\n92\t| Process exits cleanly | sanity pin for the PTY teardown contract |\n93\t\n94\tThe \"all hook events\" set from `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` (the\n95\tground-truth source of registrations) covers six events:\n96\t\n97\t| Event | Matcher | Hook script |\n98\t|---|---|---|\n99\t| `SessionStart` | (none) | `session-init.sh` |\n100\t| `SessionStart` | `*` | `freshness-session-start.sh` |\n101\t| `UserPromptSubmit` | (none) | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` |\n102\t| `PreToolUse` | `Edit\\|Write\\|mcp__serena__*` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` |\n103\t| `PostToolUse` | `Read` | `freshness-post-read.sh` (async) |\n104\t| `PostToolUse` | `mcp__auggie__.*\\|mcp__auggie-mcp__.*\\|mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__auggie_.*` | `auggie-flag-clear.sh` |\n105\t| `SubagentStart` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (async) |\n106\t| `SubagentStop` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (async) |\n107\t\n108\tSix distinct hook events appear under the top-level `hooks` key\n109\t(SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse,\n110\tSubagentStart, SubagentStop). E12 asserts presence of each.\n111\t\n112\t## 3. Frozen body shape\n113\t\n114\tThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E12 entry that\n115\tpreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\n116\ttitle `\"doctor surfaces missing claude binary\"` from the pre-OQ-2\n117\tnumbering and no body). T05.17 replaces the scaffolding with the\n118\tfrozen body. Final shape:\n119\t\n120\t| Field | Value |\n121\t|---|---|\n122\t| **title** | `\"Hook deploy idempotency\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E12) |\n123\t| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E11; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the install_hooks adapter, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n124\t| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib, no MCP, no network |\n125\t| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3..E11 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by per-eval HOME setup time + a `/quit` PTY round-trip, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n126\t| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract. No interactive work needed; the `install_hooks` invocation that produces the asserted `settings.json` is performed by the setup wrapper (FR-ISO2 / NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup), not by the in-session Claude Code process |\n127\t| **expects[0]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SessionStart, exists: true }` |\n128\t| **expects[1]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.UserPromptSubmit, exists: true }` |\n129\t| **expects[2]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PreToolUse, exists: true }` |\n130\t| **expects[3]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PostToolUse, exists: true }` |\n131\t| **expects[4]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStart, exists: true }` |\n132\t| **expects[5]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStop, exists: true }` |\n133\t| **expects[6]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n134\t\n135\tPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\n136\tscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\n137\tper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n138\t\n139\tNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — `install_hooks` is built\n140\tinto the SuperClaude CLI; no MCP server is required.\n141\t\n142\t### Schema-expressibility constraint — the \"twice\" and \"digest unchanged\" deferral\n143\t\n144\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 input shape says: \"call `install_hooks`\n145\tadapter against the per-eval HOME **twice in a row** (back-to-back\n146\tinvocations)\". The OQ-2 expect shape pairs this with: \"settings.json\n147\tfile digest unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate entries)\".\n148\t\n149\tNeither half is expressible under the current\n150\t`src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/suite.schema.json`:\n151\t\n152\t1. **No `callback:` field.** Schema `$defs/evalEntry` (lines 124-160)\n153\t   defines `id, title, category, requires, timeout_sec, isolation,\n154\t   inputs, expects, parameterize, no_pty`. There is no\n155\t   `callback:` field that could programmatically invoke\n156\t   `deploy_hooks_to` a second time from inside the eval. The D-4\n157\t   \"YAML callback escape hatch\" referenced in D-0082 §4 for E14 is\n158\t   a planned extension that has not yet been added to the schema\n159\t   (cross-referenced in `commands.py` design notes only).\n160\t2. **No `inputs[].adapter:` field.** Schema `inputs[]` items use the\n161\t   open-shape `type: object` (additionalProperties: true), but the\n162\t   runner's input dispatch only knows about `prompt` and\n163\t   `expect_tool_call` (commands.py PTY input loop). An `adapter:`\n164\t   field would be silently dropped by the runner.\n165\t3. **No `digest_unchanged:` predicate.** `Expect.settings_json`\n166\t   (`expect.py:373-480`) supports `exists` + `equals` checks on a\n167\t   `key_path` dot-traversal, but no `digest:` / `unchanged_since:`\n168\t   primitive exists in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`). The\n169\t   strict digest-unchanged form requires either a Python callable\n170\t   predicate (D-4 callback escape hatch) or a new `Expect.file.digest`\n171\t   primitive (schema bump).\n172\t\n173\tPer the established T05.07..T05.16 deferral posture, T05.17 lands the\n174\tOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n175\t\n176\t- **\"twice in a row\" proxy:** the per-eval setup wrapper invokes\n177\t  `deploy_hooks_to` exactly once (per NFR-ISO2 / T02.13). The\n178\t  asserted shape (`hooks.<event>` keys exist) holds post-setup, which\n179\t  is the \"after first deploy\" half of the OQ-2 contract. The\n180\t  \"after second deploy\" half is the deferred branch — if the same\n181\t  shape continues to hold after a hypothetical second invocation,\n182\t  the assertion as authored continues to pass (idempotency at the\n183\t  registration-presence level). The proxy is therefore **necessary\n184\t  but not sufficient**: it catches \"second deploy clobbered the\n185\t  registrations\" regressions but does not catch \"second deploy\n186\t  duplicated an entry\" regressions (the PR #49 class). The PR #49\n187\t  regression class is the explicit motivation in D-0082 §4 notes;\n188\t  catching it requires the digest-unchanged assertion.\n189\t- **\"digest unchanged\" proxy:** none — declarative DSL has no\n190\t  digest primitive. The full assertion is deferred to either (a) the\n191\t  YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) being added to the schema and\n192\t  exercised for E12, or (b) a future `Expect.file.digest` primitive.\n193\t\n194\t§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\n195\testablished by T05.07..T05.16 for the freshness-ledger emit gap.\n196\tAcceptance criteria for T05.17 are met by the describe / list /\n197\tround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\n198\tand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring\n199\tfull end-to-end `eval run --eval E12` execution depends transitively\n200\ton (i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418` and (ii) the\n201\tcallback / digest escape hatch landing.\n202\t\n203\t### Why `key_path: hooks.<event>` (not deeper)\n204\t\n205\t`Expect.settings_json` (`expect.py:373-480`) traverses dot-separated\n206\tkey segments through `Mapping` values only. The merged settings.json\n207\tstructure is:\n208\t\n209\t```jsonc\n210\t{\n211\t  \"hooks\": {\n212\t    \"SessionStart\": [\n213\t      { \"hooks\": [...] },         // unmatched entry\n214\t      { \"matcher\": \"*\", \"hooks\": [...] }  // matched entry\n215\t    ],\n216\t    \"UserPromptSubmit\": [...],\n217\t    ...\n218\t  }\n219\t}\n220\t```\n221\t\n222\t`hooks.SessionStart` resolves to the **array** of entries — an\n223\t`exists` check succeeds (the key is present at that path). Going\n224\tdeeper (e.g., `hooks.SessionStart.0.hooks.0.command`) requires array\n225\tindexing, which the current primitive does not support\n226\t(`expect.py:423-429`: traversal short-circuits at the first\n227\tnon-Mapping). The granular per-matcher pin (e.g., proving the\n228\t`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher specifically is present) is\n229\ttherefore deferred to either a future array-traversal extension or a\n230\tcallback predicate.\n231\t\n232\tFor the OQ-2 minimum AC, asserting every top-level hook event key is\n233\tpresent is sufficient to prove `install_hooks` merged the full hooks\n234\tsurface. If `install_hooks` regressed to skip an event entirely (e.g.,\n235\tSubagentStart never gets merged), the `key_path: hooks.SubagentStart`\n236\texists check would fail — that is the operationally-meaningful\n237\tregression class for the registration-presence half of the contract.\n238\t\n239\t## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n240\t\n241\t`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n242\t`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E12` is\n243\ttrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` returns\n244\tthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\n245\tunder suite `real`.\n246\t\n247\t## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n248\t\n249\tThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\n250\tconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n251\t\n252\t- The setup wrapper invokes `deploy_hooks_to` against the\n253\t  freshly-created per-eval HOME, which in turn calls `install_hooks`\n254\t  + the verbatim `hooks.json` copy. `install_hooks` is itself\n255\t  deterministic given a clean `settings.json` target — the matcher-\n256\t  collision detection (install_hooks.py merge logic) short-circuits\n257\t  on a fresh HOME (no prior settings.json), producing a deterministic\n258\t  output identical to the source `hooks.json` registrations.\n259\t- The `Expect.settings_json` assertions are pure-read on the\n260\t  resulting `<home>/.claude/settings.json` — no time-of-day, network,\n261\t  or shared-state dependencies. Six independent `exists` checks\n262\t  against six fixed key_paths.\n263\t- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n264\t  after PTY harness EOF.\n265\t- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n266\t  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n267\t\n268\tThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E12` invocations on\n269\ta clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\n270\twhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The \"twice in a row\"\n271\tstrict form remains deferred; the determinism of the registration-\n272\tpresence proxy is independent of the deferral.)\n273\t\n274\t## 6. Schema validation\n275\t\n276\tThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n277\t\n278\t- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n279\t  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The single-element\n280\t  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema.\n281\t- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n282\t  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n283\t  primitives used (6×`settings_json`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n284\t  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n285\t- `settings_json` primitive kwargs `path`, `key_path`, `exists` are\n286\t  supported by `Expect.settings_json._build` (`expect.py:373-480`).\n287\t- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n288\t  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n289\t- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n290\t- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n291\t- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n292\t- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n293\t\n294\tNo schema-version bump required.\n295\t\n296\t## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n297\t\n298\t| Invocation | E12 outcome | Why |\n299\t|---|---|---|\n300\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E12` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n301\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib |\n302\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n303\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n304\t\n305\tThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E11 (which also carry\n306\t`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\n307\tcapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n308\t\n309\t## 8. Verification\n310\t\n311\tPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.17, primary verifier:\n312\t\n313\t```bash\n314\tuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E12\n315\t```\n316\t\n317\t**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n318\t'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\n319\tdocumented in T05.03 / T05.04 / T05.05 / T05.07..T05.16 evidence\n320\tblocks all block any direct `eval run` invocation. That blocker is\n321\tthe responsibility of the runner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency\n322\tof the CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint at T05.18). T05.17 authors the\n323\tmanifest body; observable verification is therefore via:\n324\t\n325\t- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` rendering the new\n326\t  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n327\t  `evidence/T05.17/describe-E12.txt`);\n328\t- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n329\t  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n330\t  `evidence/T05.17/list-with-E12.txt`);\n331\t- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n332\t  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n333\t  `evidence/T05.17/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n334\t\n335\tFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls\n336\tinto the runner-completion task downstream of T05.17.\n337\t\n338\t### 8.1 Deferred branches — \"twice in a row\" and \"digest unchanged\"\n339\t\n340\tOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 specifies a two-part contract:\n341\t\n342\t1. **Registration presence.** After the install_hooks adapter runs,\n343\t   every hook event from the source `hooks.json` must be registered\n344\t   in the per-eval HOME's `settings.json`. **Landed verbatim** by\n345\t   T05.17 via the six `Expect.settings_json(key_path=hooks.<event>,\n346\t   exists=true)` rows.\n347\t2. **Idempotency under re-invocation.** A second back-to-back\n348\t   `install_hooks` call must produce **byte-identical** settings.json\n349\t   (no duplicated matcher entries, no clobbered registrations). The\n350\t   PR #49 regression class is exactly: \"matcher exists but a second\n351\t   deploy duplicates it\". **Deferred** by T05.17 — declarative DSL\n352\t   has no callback hook to invoke the adapter twice, and no\n353\t   `digest_unchanged` predicate to compare pre/post-second-deploy\n354\t   states.\n355\t\n356\tFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.16 (telemetry gaps in\n357\tfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\n358\tdeclaratively, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.17 lands the\n359\tOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy for branch (1)\n360\tand defers branch (2) to a follow-up task gated on either:\n361\t\n362\t- (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) being added to the\n363\t  schema and exercised for E12 — the callback would invoke\n364\t  `deploy_hooks_to(home_path)` a second time and compare SHA256\n365\t  digests of the pre/post settings.json;\n366\t- (b) a future schema bump adding declarative\n367\t  `expect.file.digest_unchanged_after: { adapter_call: ... }` or\n368\t  equivalent shorthand.\n369\t\n370\tNeither is in scope for T05.17.\n371\t\n372\tThe proxy retains operational meaning: if `install_hooks` regresses\n373\tsuch that a hook event is dropped from the merge pipeline (e.g.,\n374\tSubagentStart gets skipped on second deploy), the registration-\n375\tpresence assertion would catch it on a follow-up describe/expect\n376\tround-trip OR on a re-run after the runner fix lands. The strict\n377\tPR #49 regression class is on the **digest-unchanged** branch\n378\tspecifically.\n379\t\n380\tThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.17; it predates the deliverable\n381\tand is structural to the declarative DSL's expressibility envelope.\n382\tAcceptance criteria for T05.17 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\n383\tid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\n384\tby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\n385\tthat requires `eval run --eval E12` to exit 0 deterministically\n386\tdepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix and (b) the\n387\t\"twice + digest\" deferred branches if/when the user demands strict\n388\tPR #49 regression coverage.\n389\t\n390\t### 8.2 Setup-wrapper dependency\n391\t\n392\tE12 is unique in the post-OQ-2 hook-coverage roster in that its\n393\tassertions read **only** the merged settings.json — not any\n394\truntime-emitted freshness ledger. This means the eval's correctness\n395\tdepends entirely on the per-eval setup wrapper invoking\n396\t`deploy_hooks_to` correctly. If the setup wrapper is missing or\n397\tbroken (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13 incomplete), the per-eval HOME has no\n398\t`.claude/settings.json`, all six `settings_json.exists` checks would\n399\treport \"settings.json not found at <path>\", and the eval would\n400\tERROR.\n401\t\n402\tThis is the correct failure mode: an E12 ERROR (not FAIL) under\n403\t\"settings.json not found\" surfaces a regression in the setup wrapper,\n404\twhich is precisely what E12 implicitly verifies. Sibling E3-E11\n405\tevals depend on the same wrapper (their freshness-ledger assertions\n406\tfail open if the wrapper doesn't deploy the hooks at all), but they\n407\tdo not specifically pin the wrapper's correctness — E12 does.\n408\t\n409\tThis makes E12 the **integration smoke test** for the install_hooks\n410\tadapter's idempotency contract AND the per-eval setup wrapper's\n411\tcorrectness. The two failure modes are distinguishable by the error\n412\ttag: \"settings.json not found\" → wrapper bug; \"hooks.<event> exists\n413\tmismatch\" → install_hooks bug.\n414\t\n415\t## 9. Impacts / dependencies\n416\t\n417\t| Direction | Item | Note |\n418\t|---|---|---|\n419\t| Depends on | T05.01 / D-0082 | OQ-2 resolution — frozen body shape |\n420\t| Depends on | T01.05 (FR-SCH2 validate_eval_id) | accepts literal `E12` |\n421\t| Depends on | T04.06 / T04.07 (Expect.settings_json impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py:373-480` |\n422\t| Depends on | T04.04 / T04.05 (Expect.exit_code impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py` |\n423\t| Depends on | T02.14 / D-0034 (`hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`) | satisfied by current `hook_adapter.py:183-256` |\n424\t| Depends on | T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper) | wrapper invokes `deploy_hooks_to` from inside HomeIsolation.setup; required for E12 assertions to find a settings.json at all |\n425\t| Sibling | T05.07..T05.16 (E3..E11 bodies) | E12 is the ninth and final post-OQ-2 hook-coverage body; shares `category: hook-lifecycle`, `requires: []`, `timeout_sec: 60`, `/quit` exit pattern |\n426\t| Differs from | T05.07..T05.16 (E3..E11 bodies) | E12's surface is the adapter, not a hook script; reads settings.json, not freshness.jsonl; minimal inputs (no in-session work) |\n427\t| Unblocks | T05.18 (CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint) | E12 must enumerate + describe; full-run verification follows runner fix |\n428\t| Unblocks | follow-up \"twice + digest\" task | gated on D-4 callback escape hatch landing |\n429\t\n430\t## 10. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E12 — the ninth and\nfinal post-OQ-2 hook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098) before the\nPhase-5 R3 mitigation block (T05.19+). E12 covers the\n**`install_hooks` adapter idempotency contract** documented at\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py:28-33` (\"Re-invoking\n`deploy_hooks_to` against the same `home_path` produces identical\nfilesystem state\").\n\nE12 is structurally distinct from E3-E11 along two axes:\n\n1. **Cross-cutting surface (not a single hook).** E3-E11 each exercise\n   one hook event entry from `hooks.json`. E12's surface is the\n   **deployer** (`hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`), so the eval reads\n   the merged `settings.json` rather than the `logs/freshness.jsonl`\n   ledger.\n2. **Not PTY-driven.** E3-E11 inject `/quit`-bounded prompt sequences\n   through the PTY harness so the in-session Claude Code process\n   fires the matched hook. E12 has no in-session work to do — the\n   adapter call is performed by the harness during HOME setup\n   (`HomeIsolation.setup` → `deploy_hooks_to` per the NFR-ISO2 wiring\n   task), and the eval only asserts the post-setup `settings.json`\n   shape. The body therefore lands with a minimal `/quit`-only\n   inputs sequence and leans on `Expect.settings_json` rather than\n   `Expect.file`.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the\n  `deploy_hooks_to` call invoked by the setup wrapper is the\n  effective \"first deploy\" of the OQ-2 contract;\n- assert that the merged `settings.json` carries hook registrations\n  for **every** hook event in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` —\n  the `has_registration(<all hook events>, <all matchers>)` predicate\n  from OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12, mapped to existing\n  `Expect.settings_json` primitive checks (mapping documented in §3\n  and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E11;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — `install_hooks` is a\n  pure stdlib operation; no MCP, no network.\n\nThe \"twice in a row\" element of the OQ-2 input shape (and its paired\n`digest unchanged` expect) cannot be expressed in the current\ndeclarative DSL (the schema has no `callback:` field — see §3\n\"Schema-expressibility constraint\" below). The strict declarative form\nis deferred to a follow-up task per the established T05.07..T05.16\nprecedent: land the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\ndeclarative proxies, document the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict\nform on the YAML callback escape hatch / a future\n`adapter:` schema extension.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hook_adapter.py` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py:183-256` `deploy_hooks_to`:\n\n```python\ndef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Deploy SuperClaude hooks into the per-eval HOME at ``home_path``.\n\n    Two filesystem effects, in this order:\n    1. install_hooks() copies hook scripts to <home>/.claude/hooks/\n       and merges hook registrations into <home>/.claude/settings.json.\n    2. Source src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json is copied verbatim to\n       <home>/.claude/hooks.json (byte-identical via shutil.copy2).\n\n    Idempotent: re-invocation on the same home_path produces identical\n    filesystem state.\n    \"\"\"\n```\n\nThe idempotency contract of interest (OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12):\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the first deploy | proves the install_hooks merge pipeline produced the expected target shape |\n| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the second deploy | proves the second deploy did NOT remove/break the registrations |\n| The settings.json digest is unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate matcher entries) | proves the second deploy is a true no-op — the bug class from PR #49 was exactly this: matcher exists but a second deploy duplicated it |\n| Process exits cleanly | sanity pin for the PTY teardown contract |\n\nThe \"all hook events\" set from `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` (the\nground-truth source of registrations) covers six events:\n\n| Event | Matcher | Hook script |\n|---|---|---|\n| `SessionStart` | (none) | `session-init.sh` |\n| `SessionStart` | `*` | `freshness-session-start.sh` |\n| `UserPromptSubmit` | (none) | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` |\n| `PreToolUse` | `Edit\\|Write\\|mcp__serena__*` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` |\n| `PostToolUse` | `Read` | `freshness-post-read.sh` (async) |\n| `PostToolUse` | `mcp__auggie__.*\\|mcp__auggie-mcp__.*\\|mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__auggie_.*` | `auggie-flag-clear.sh` |\n| `SubagentStart` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (async) |\n| `SubagentStop` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (async) |\n\nSix distinct hook events appear under the top-level `hooks` key\n(SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse,\nSubagentStart, SubagentStop). E12 asserts presence of each.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E12 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle `\"doctor surfaces missing claude binary\"` from the pre-OQ-2\nnumbering and no body). T05.17 replaces the scaffolding with the\nfrozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Hook deploy idempotency\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E12) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E11; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the install_hooks adapter, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib, no MCP, no network |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3..E11 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by per-eval HOME setup time + a `/quit` PTY round-trip, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract. No interactive work needed; the `install_hooks` invocation that produces the asserted `settings.json` is performed by the setup wrapper (FR-ISO2 / NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup), not by the in-session Claude Code process |\n| **expects[0]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SessionStart, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.UserPromptSubmit, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PreToolUse, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[3]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PostToolUse, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[4]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStart, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[5]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStop, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[6]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — `install_hooks` is built\ninto the SuperClaude CLI; no MCP server is required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the \"twice\" and \"digest unchanged\" deferral\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 input shape says: \"call `install_hooks`\nadapter against the per-eval HOME **twice in a row** (back-to-back\ninvocations)\". The OQ-2 expect shape pairs this with: \"settings.json\nfile digest unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate entries)\".\n\nNeither half is expressible under the current\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/suite.schema.json`:\n\n1. **No `callback:` field.** Schema `$defs/evalEntry` (lines 124-160)\n   defines `id, title, category, requires, timeout_sec, isolation,\n   inputs, expects, parameterize, no_pty`. There is no\n   `callback:` field that could programmatically invoke\n   `deploy_hooks_to` a second time from inside the eval. The D-4\n   \"YAML callback escape hatch\" referenced in D-0082 §4 for E14 is\n   a planned extension that has not yet been added to the schema\n   (cross-referenced in `commands.py` design notes only).\n2. **No `inputs[].adapter:` field.** Schema `inputs[]` items use the\n   open-shape `type: object` (additionalProperties: true), but the\n   runner's input dispatch only knows about `prompt` and\n   `expect_tool_call` (commands.py PTY input loop). An `adapter:`\n   field would be silently dropped by the runner.\n3. **No `digest_unchanged:` predicate.** `Expect.settings_json`\n   (`expect.py:373-480`) supports `exists` + `equals` checks on a\n   `key_path` dot-traversal, but no `digest:` / `unchanged_since:`\n   primitive exists in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`). The\n   strict digest-unchanged form requires either a Python callable\n   predicate (D-4 callback escape hatch) or a new `Expect.file.digest`\n   primitive (schema bump).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.16 deferral posture, T05.17 lands the\nOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **\"twice in a row\" proxy:** the per-eval setup wrapper invokes\n  `deploy_hooks_to` exactly once (per NFR-ISO2 / T02.13). The\n  asserted shape (`hooks.<event>` keys exist) holds post-setup, which\n  is the \"after first deploy\" half of the OQ-2 contract. The\n  \"after second deploy\" half is the deferred branch — if the same\n  shape continues to hold after a hypothetical second invocation,\n  the assertion as authored continues to pass (idempotency at the\n  registration-presence level). The proxy is therefore **necessary\n  but not sufficient**: it catches \"second deploy clobbered the\n  registrations\" regressions but does not catch \"second deploy\n  duplicated an entry\" regressions (the PR #49 class). The PR #49\n  regression class is the explicit motivation in D-0082 §4 notes;\n  catching it requires the digest-unchanged assertion.\n- **\"digest unchanged\" proxy:** none — declarative DSL has no\n  digest primitive. The full assertion is deferred to either (a) the\n  YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) being added to the schema and\n  exercised for E12, or (b) a future `Expect.file.digest` primitive.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\nestablished by T05.07..T05.16 for the freshness-ledger emit gap.\nAcceptance criteria for T05.17 are met by the describe / list /\nround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\nand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring\nfull end-to-end `eval run --eval E12` execution depends transitively\non (i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418` and (ii) the\ncallback / digest escape hatch landing.\n\n### Why `key_path: hooks.<event>` (not deeper)\n\n`Expect.settings_json` (`expect.py:373-480`) traverses dot-separated\nkey segments through `Mapping` values only. The merged settings.json\nstructure is:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"hooks\": {\n    \"SessionStart\": [\n      { \"hooks\": [...] },         // unmatched entry\n      { \"matcher\": \"*\", \"hooks\": [...] }  // matched entry\n    ],\n    \"UserPromptSubmit\": [...],\n    ...\n  }\n}\n```\n\n`hooks.SessionStart` resolves to the **array** of entries — an\n`exists` check succeeds (the key is present at that path). Going\ndeeper (e.g., `hooks.SessionStart.0.hooks.0.command`) requires array\nindexing, which the current primitive does not support\n(`expect.py:423-429`: traversal short-circuits at the first\nnon-Mapping). The granular per-matcher pin (e.g., proving the\n`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher specifically is present) is\ntherefore deferred to either a future array-traversal extension or a\ncallback predicate.\n\nFor the OQ-2 minimum AC, asserting every top-level hook event key is\npresent is sufficient to prove `install_hooks` merged the full hooks\nsurface. If `install_hooks` regressed to skip an event entirely (e.g.,\nSubagentStart never gets merged), the `key_path: hooks.SubagentStart`\nexists check would fail — that is the operationally-meaningful\nregression class for the registration-presence half of the contract.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E12` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The setup wrapper invokes `deploy_hooks_to` against the\n  freshly-created per-eval HOME, which in turn calls `install_hooks`\n  + the verbatim `hooks.json` copy. `install_hooks` is itself\n  deterministic given a clean `settings.json` target — the matcher-\n  collision detection (install_hooks.py merge logic) short-circuits\n  on a fresh HOME (no prior settings.json), producing a deterministic\n  output identical to the source `hooks.json` registrations.\n- The `Expect.settings_json` assertions are pure-read on the\n  resulting `<home>/.claude/settings.json` — no time-of-day, network,\n  or shared-state dependencies. Six independent `exists` checks\n  against six fixed key_paths.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E12` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The \"twice in a row\"\nstrict form remains deferred; the determinism of the registration-\npresence proxy is independent of the deferral.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The single-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema.\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (6×`settings_json`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `settings_json` primitive kwargs `path`, `key_path`, `exists` are\n  supported by `Expect.settings_json._build` (`expect.py:373-480`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E12 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E11 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.17, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E12\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03 / T05.04 / T05.05 / T05.07..T05.16 evidence\nblocks all block any direct `eval run` invocation. That blocker is\nthe responsibility of the runner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency\nof the CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint at T05.18). T05.17 authors the\nmanifest body; observable verification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/describe-E12.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/list-with-E12.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls\ninto the runner-completion task downstream of T05.17.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — \"twice in a row\" and \"digest unchanged\"\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 specifies a two-part contract:\n\n1. **Registration presence.** After the install_hooks adapter runs,\n   every hook event from the source `hooks.json` must be registered\n   in the per-eval HOME's `settings.json`. **Landed verbatim** by\n   T05.17 via the six `Expect.settings_json(key_path=hooks.<event>,\n   exists=true)` rows.\n2. **Idempotency under re-invocation.** A second back-to-back\n   `install_hooks` call must produce **byte-identical** settings.json\n   (no duplicated matcher entries, no clobbered registrations). The\n   PR #49 regression class is exactly: \"matcher exists but a second\n   deploy duplicates it\". **Deferred** by T05.17 — declarative DSL\n   has no callback hook to invoke the adapter twice, and no\n   `digest_unchanged` predicate to compare pre/post-second-deploy\n   states.\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.16 (telemetry gaps in\nfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\ndeclaratively, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.17 lands the\nOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy for branch (1)\nand defers branch (2) to a follow-up task gated on either:\n\n- (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) being added to the\n  schema and exercised for E12 — the callback would invoke\n  `deploy_hooks_to(home_path)` a second time and compare SHA256\n  digests of the pre/post settings.json;\n- (b) a future schema bump adding declarative\n  `expect.file.digest_unchanged_after: { adapter_call: ... }` or\n  equivalent shorthand.\n\nNeither is in scope for T05.17.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: if `install_hooks` regresses\nsuch that a hook event is dropped from the merge pipeline (e.g.,\nSubagentStart gets skipped on second deploy), the registration-\npresence assertion would catch it on a follow-up describe/expect\nround-trip OR on a re-run after the runner fix lands. The strict\nPR #49 regression class is on the **digest-unchanged** branch\nspecifically.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.17; it predates the deliverable\nand is structural to the declarative DSL's expressibility envelope.\nAcceptance criteria for T05.17 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\nid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\nby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\nthat requires `eval run --eval E12` to exit 0 deterministically\ndepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix and (b) the\n\"twice + digest\" deferred branches if/when the user demands strict\nPR #49 regression coverage.\n\n### 8.2 Setup-wrapper dependency\n\nE12 is unique in the post-OQ-2 hook-coverage roster in that its\nassertions read **only** the merged settings.json — not any\nruntime-emitted freshness ledger. This means the eval's correctness\ndepends entirely on the per-eval setup wrapper invoking\n`deploy_hooks_to` correctly. If the setup wrapper is missing or\nbroken (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13 incomplete), the per-eval HOME has no\n`.claude/settings.json`, all six `settings_json.exists` checks would\nreport \"settings.json not found at <path>\", and the eval would\nERROR.\n\nThis is the correct failure mode: an E12 ERROR (not FAIL) under\n\"settings.json not found\" surfaces a regression in the setup wrapper,\nwhich is precisely what E12 implicitly verifies. Sibling E3-E11\nevals depend on the same wrapper (their freshness-ledger assertions\nfail open if the wrapper doesn't deploy the hooks at all), but they\ndo not specifically pin the wrapper's correctness — E12 does.\n\nThis makes E12 the **integration smoke test** for the install_hooks\nadapter's idempotency contract AND the per-eval setup wrapper's\ncorrectness. The two failure modes are distinguishable by the error\ntag: \"settings.json not found\" → wrapper bug; \"hooks.<event> exists\nmismatch\" → install_hooks bug.\n\n## 9. The harness must\n> (a) complete the matched tool call successfully, (b) capture the\n> hook's stderr to the eval transcript so a human reader can see what\n> went wrong, and (c) emit a structured `{type:\"hook_error\",\n> disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` so the\n> failure is auditable post-run.\n\nThe regression class motivating E13 is: a misbehaving hook script\nsurfaces its own stderr as if the matched TOOL had failed — the\nagent sees the hook's error message instead of the tool's result.\nThis is the \"hook stderr causes apparent tool failure\" bug noted in\nthe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13.\n\nE13 is structurally distinct from E3-E12 along two axes:\n\n1. **Cross-cutting surface (the harness, not a single hook).** E3-E11\n   each exercise one hook event entry from `hooks.json`; E12 covers\n   the install_hooks adapter; E13 covers the **harness's error-\n   handling path** — the code that runs when ANY hook returns\n   non-zero. The eval is hook-event-agnostic by design (a failing\n   PostToolUse Read hook happens to be the concrete fixture, but\n   the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing PreToolUse\n   Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook).\n2. **Requires a hooks.json variant deployment.** Unlike E3-E12, which\n   all run against the production `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`\n   shipped via `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`, E13 requires a\n   **test-only** hooks.json variant that registers the failing\n   fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) as the\n   PostToolUse Read handler — otherwise the matched tool call would\n   fire the production `freshness-post-read.sh` (which exits cleanly)\n   and no hook error would surface. See §3 \"Scaffolding gap\" for\n   the deferral.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the harness's\n  error-logging path writes to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  which must not exist prior to the run;\n- assert the harness's three-part fail-open contract (tool call\n  succeeds, stderr captured, structured error row emitted) — mapped\n  to the available declarative DSL primitives (mapping documented\n  in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E12;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the failing fixture is\n  a pure shell script; no MCP, no network.\n\nThe \"hooks.json variant deployment\" + the \"structured `{type,\ndisposition}` event-count predicate\" cannot be expressed in the\ncurrent declarative DSL. The strict declarative form is deferred to\nfollow-up tasks per the established T05.07..T05.17 precedent: land\nthe OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort declarative proxies,\ndocument the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the YAML\ncallback escape hatch / a future schema extension.\n\n## 2. Hook error-path contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's hook-execution loop (commands.py / runner.py hook-exec\npath) wraps each hook script invocation and is responsible for the\nfail-open discipline:\n\n```\nFor each hook in hooks.json matching the current event:\n  1. Spawn hook script with timeout from `timeout:` field.\n  2. Capture stdout (envelope-parsed), stderr (line-captured), and\n     exit code.\n  3. If exit code != 0:\n     a. The tool call MUST continue (fail-open) — the hook does NOT\n        block the matched tool's result from reaching the agent.\n     b. The hook's stderr MUST be propagated to the eval transcript\n        (so the human reader sees what went wrong).\n     c. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\",\n        hook:<script-name>, exit_code:<n>, stderr:<truncated>, ...}`\n        row MUST be appended to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`.\n  4. If exit code == 0:\n     normal-path telemetry only (no hook-errors.jsonl row).\n```\n\nThe fixture script (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13) is\n`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` — a deterministic-exit\nshell script that prints a known message to stderr and exits with\na non-zero code. The eval registers it as the PostToolUse Read\nhandler in a test-only hooks.json variant; the inputs trigger a\nRead; the harness fires the failing hook; the harness logs the\nerror and fails open; the assertions pin all three observable\nguarantees.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists post-run | proves the harness opened the error ledger at all |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"hook_error\"` substring | proves the harness emitted a hook_error-typed row |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"` substring | proves the disposition was fail-open (vs. fail-closed) |\n| `stderr` contains `\"failing-post-read.sh\"` | proves the hook's stderr was captured + surfaced to the eval transcript |\n| Process exits cleanly (exit_code == 0) | proves the failing hook did NOT propagate up to the eval-process exit code (the fail-open contract decouples hook exit from process exit) |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E13 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor surfaces\nstale ~/.claude/settings.json schema\"`, `category: doctor` — left\nover from the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was a doctor-surface eval).\nT05.19 replaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Hook stderr error fails open\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E13) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E12; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the harness's hook error-handling path, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the failing fixture is a pure shell script, no MCP, no network |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E12 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by 1 Write + 1 Read + /quit through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | seed `fixture.txt` via Write so the subsequent Read has a target (mirrors E9's fixture-seeding pattern) |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | trigger a Read of `fixture.txt`; this is the call whose PostToolUse hook (the failing fixture) returns non-zero — the surface this eval asserts; carries `expect_tool_call: Read` to pin the matched tool |\n| **inputs[2].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"hook_error\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"' }` |\n| **expects[3]** | `stderr: { contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\" }` |\n| **expects[4]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the failing fixture is a\npure shell script; no MCP server is required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `contains_event(type=..., disposition=...)` proxy\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expect shape names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass. The proxy is **necessary\n  but not sufficient** for the strict semantic: it does not prove\n  the two substrings appeared on the SAME JSONL row (a hypothetical\n  pathological emit pattern that wrote `{type:hook_error,\n  disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and `{type:other_thing,\n  disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would satisfy the\n  proxies but violate the strict semantic). In practice the\n  harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\n  row by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\n  the strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\n  across rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing.\n- **Event-count proxy:** none — the OQ-2 shape doesn't require\n  `event_count == 1`, only `contains_event` (at-least-once), so\n  the substring presence proxy is a complete coverage of the\n  at-least-once aspect.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives, matching mechanism (Expect.file with JSONL\nsubstring), matching deferral footprint (declarative DSL\nexpressibility limitation). The conjunctive `{type, matcher}`\nshape converges with the strict semantic for the same reason\ndescribed above (the harness emits all matcher-pin fields on a\nsingle row by design).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — the failing fixture + hooks.json variant deferral\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires a **test-only hooks.json variant**\nthat registers `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` as the\nPostToolUse Read handler. Three preconditions are needed:\n\n1. **The fixture script `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`\n   must exist on disk.** Today it does not (per the codebase grep:\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory does not exist; no\n   `failing-post-read*` file anywhere in the repo). A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the fixture as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **A hooks.json-variant deployment path must exist.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` field on `evalEntry` and no\n   `inputs[].setup:` callback that could swap in a test-only\n   hooks.json with the failing fixture registered. Two options\n   for landing this:\n   - (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) — a callback that\n     writes a test-only hooks.json to `<home>/.claude/hooks.json`\n     and `<home>/.claude/settings.json` before the PTY session\n     opens;\n   - (b) a new `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field that\n     points to a test-only hooks.json fixture, with the setup\n     wrapper preferring the variant over the production hooks.json\n     when present.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger must be wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is responsible for\n   wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on the\n   non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nUntil all three land, E13's full end-to-end execution path\n(`eval run --suite real --eval E13`) will surface predictable\nfailures: the harness deploys the production hooks.json (no\nfailing fixture is registered), no PostToolUse hook fails, no\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` is written, and the eval ERRORs with\n\"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\". This is the correct\nfailure mode — surfacing each missing precondition incrementally.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\nestablished by T05.07..T05.17. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 are\nmet by the describe / list / round-trip evidence (the manifest body\nis FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped, and resolves through\n`Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring full end-to-end\n`eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on (i) the\nrunner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired.\n\n### Why `expect_tool_call: Read` (not just a free-form prompt)\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\n### Why a seed Write before the Read\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse hook on the Write matcher\nbranch (covered independently by E7) — that co-fire is harmless\nbecause E13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by\n`type:\"hook_error\"` on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit\ntelemetry on `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E13` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17\nevals under suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The failing fixture (once landed per §3 scaffolding gap) is a\n  deterministic-exit shell script — same exit code + same stderr\n  message on every invocation, with no time-of-day or network\n  dependencies.\n- The harness's error-logging path (once wired) is a pure-append to\n  `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` — no shared-state dependencies, no\n  ordering ambiguity (the failing hook fires once per matched Read,\n  the eval triggers exactly one Read, so the ledger contains exactly\n  one error row).\n- The asserted substrings (`\"type\":\"hook_error\"`,\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`, `\"failing-post-read.sh\"`) are\n  invariant across runs (the `ts`, `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`,\n  `stderr` (truncated copy) fields on the JSONL row, once emitted,\n  are not asserted against).\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E13` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The determinism\ncontract is independent of the scaffolding gap — once the gap\ncloses, the body is determined by construction.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` and `inputs[].expect_tool_call: string`\n  (additionalProperties: true under `evalEntry.inputs.items` per\n  `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved\n  at load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (3×`file`, 1×`stderr`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `stderr` primitive kwarg `contains` is supported by\n  `Expect.stderr._build` (`expect.py:556-569`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E13 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 scaffolding gap) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; failing fixture is pure shell |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.19, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E13\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03..T05.17 evidence blocks all block any direct\n`eval run` invocation. That blocker is the responsibility of the\nrunner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency of the CP-P05-T13-T17\ncheckpoint at T05.18). T05.19 authors the manifest body; observable\nverification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/describe-E13.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/list-with-E13.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls into\nthe runner-completion task downstream of T05.19, AND the\nscaffolding-gap closure tasks described in §8.1.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — scaffolding gap + contains_event predicate\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 specifies a four-part contract:\n\n1. **Tool call completes successfully (fail-open).** Landed verbatim\n   via `exit_code.equals(0)` — the PTY-teardown exit code being 0\n   is the necessary downstream observable for \"the failing hook\n   did not propagate up to the eval-process exit code\". (Strict\n   form would also assert the Read tool's specific result reached\n   the agent; deferred to a future per-tool-result primitive.)\n2. **Stderr captured.** Landed verbatim via\n   `stderr.contains(\"failing-post-read.sh\")` — the OQ-2-named\n   `stderr.contains(failing_hook_script_name)` assertion, with\n   the script name literalized from the OQ-2-named fixture path.\n3. **Hook-error ledger row present with `{type:hook_error,\n   disposition:fail_open}` fields.** Landed with the declarative\n   proxy: three `Expect.file` rows on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n   (one for existence, one for each of the two substring pins).\n   Strict `Expect.jsonl.contains_event(type=hook_error,\n   disposition=fail_open)` requires a Python callable filter\n   (expect.py:269-369), not expressible in declarative YAML.\n4. **The harness wires all of the above.** **Deferred** — the\n   harness today does not (a) deploy a hooks.json variant with the\n   failing fixture, (b) emit a structured hook-error ledger on\n   non-zero hook exit, or (c) include a fixture script at\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`. See §3 \"Scaffolding\n   gap\" for the three sub-deferrals.\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.17 (telemetry gaps in\nfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\ndeclaratively, \"twice + digest\" deferrals), T05.19 lands the OQ-2\nbody shape with the best-effort declarative proxies for branches\n(1)-(3) and defers branch (4) to follow-up tasks gated on:\n\n- (a) the failing fixture script landing at\n  `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`;\n- (b) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) OR a new\n  `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field, plus the per-eval\n  setup wrapper honoring the variant deployment;\n- (c) the harness's structured hook-error ledger being wired into\n  the hook-exec path (commands.py / runner.py) to emit\n  `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\", hook:..., exit_code:...,\n  stderr:..., ts:..., session_id:..., tool_call_idx:...}` rows to\n  `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl` on non-zero hook exit;\n- (d) a future schema bump adding declarative\n  `jsonl.contains_event:` primitive shorthand (optional — the\n  two-substring proxy is operationally meaningful as documented\n  in §3 above).\n\n(d) is optional because the two-substring proxy converges with the\nstrict semantic in the absence of pathological emit patterns; (a)-(c)\nare required for ANY end-to-end execution of E13 to pass at all.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: once (a)-(c) land, the body\ncatches every regression in the harness's fail-open discipline —\nmissing ledger file, wrong type field, wrong disposition field,\nswallowed stderr, propagated hook exit code. The strict\n`contains_event` form (d) only adds discrimination against\npathological emit-patterns that split the two pinned fields across\nmultiple JSONL rows, which is itself an emit-pattern bug worth\nsurfacing through other channels.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.19; it predates the\ndeliverable and is structural to the declarative DSL's\nexpressibility envelope PLUS the harness's current error-handling\nmaturity. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 (manifest body landed,\nFR-SCH2-valid id, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence\nwritten) are met by the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above;\nthe per-task AC that requires `eval run --eval E13` to exit 0\ndeterministically depends transitively on (i) the runner NameError\nfix and (ii)-(iv) the four scaffolding-gap closures listed in §3.\n\n### 8.2 Failure-mode taxonomy\n\n| Failure mode | Surface | Discriminator |\n|---|---|---|\n| Runner NameError unblocked, fixture missing | eval ERROR | \"hook script not found at tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh\" (or hooks.json validation error) |\n| Fixture exists, hooks.json variant deployment missing | eval FAIL on expects[0..2] | \"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\" — production hooks.json deployed, no hook fails, no ledger row emitted |\n| Variant deployment wired, ledger emission missing | eval FAIL on expects[0..2] | \"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\" — failing hook fires but harness doesn't emit structured error row |\n| Ledger emission wired, wrong type | eval FAIL on expects[1] | \"file does not contain '\\\"type\\\":\\\"hook_error\\\"'\" — type field mislabeled |\n| Ledger emission wired, fail-closed regression | eval FAIL on expects[2] | \"file does not contain '\\\"disposition\\\":\\\"fail_open\\\"'\" — disposition field wrong (suggests fail-closed regression that would also fail expects[4] via non-zero exit) |\n| Ledger emission wired, stderr swallowed | eval FAIL on expects[3] | \"stderr does not contain 'failing-post-read.sh'\" — hook's stderr captured but not propagated to eval transcript |\n| Ledger emission wired, hook exit propagated | eval FAIL on expects[4] | \"exit_code != 0\" — failing hook's exit code propagated up to eval process (fail-open contract violation, the dominant regression class) |\n\nThe expects[] ordering is deliberate: expects[0..2] surface the\nstrongest scaffolding gap (missing ledger) first; expects[3..4]\ndiscriminate the subtler regressions (swallowed stderr vs.\npropagated exit code).\n\n## 9.
