Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E13 — the tenth post-OQ-2\nhook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the harness's\n**hook stderr error-path discipline** (design-spec §11 / OQ-2 D-0082\n§4 row E13). E13 exercises the documented fail-open contract:\n\n> A hook script that returns a non-zero exit code with content on\n> stderr MUST NOT cause an apparent tool failure. The harness must\n> (a) complete the matched tool call successfully, (b) capture the\n> hook's stderr to the eval transcript so a human reader can see what\n> went wrong, and (c) emit a structured `{type:\"hook_error\",\n> disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` so the\n> failure is auditable post-run.\n\nThe regression class motivating E13 is: a misbehaving hook script\nsurfaces its own stderr as if the matched TOOL had failed — the\nagent sees the hook's error message instead of the tool's result.\nThis is the \"hook stderr causes apparent tool failure\" bug noted in\nthe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13.\n\nE13 is structurally distinct from E3-E12 along two axes:\n\n1. **Cross-cutting surface (the harness, not a single hook).** E3-E11\n   each exercise one hook event entry from `hooks.json`; E12 covers\n   the install_hooks adapter; E13 covers the **harness's error-\n   handling path** — the code that runs when ANY hook returns\n   non-zero. The eval is hook-event-agnostic by design (a failing\n   PostToolUse Read hook happens to be the concrete fixture, but\n   the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing PreToolUse\n   Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook).\n2. **Requires a hooks.json variant deployment.** Unlike E3-E12, which\n   all run against the production `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`\n   shipped via `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`, E13 requires a\n   **test-only** hooks.json variant that registers the failing\n   fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) as the\n   PostToolUse Read handler — otherwise the matched tool call would\n   fire the production `freshness-post-read.sh` (which exits cleanly)\n   and no hook error would surface. See §3 \"Scaffolding gap\" for\n   the deferral.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the harness's\n  error-logging path writes to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  which must not exist prior to the run;\n- assert the harness's three-part fail-open contract (tool call\n  succeeds, stderr captured, structured error row emitted) — mapped\n  to the available declarative DSL primitives (mapping documented\n  in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E12;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the failing fixture is\n  a pure shell script; no MCP, no network.\n\nThe \"hooks.json variant deployment\" + the \"structured `{type,\ndisposition}` event-count predicate\" cannot be expressed in the\ncurrent declarative DSL. The strict declarative form is deferred to\nfollow-up tasks per the established T05.07..T05.17 precedent: land\nthe OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort declarative proxies,\ndocument the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the YAML\ncallback escape hatch / a future schema extension.\n\n## 2. Hook error-path contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's hook-execution loop (commands.py / runner.py hook-exec\npath) wraps each hook script invocation and is responsible for the\nfail-open discipline:\n\n```\nFor each hook in hooks.json matching the current event:\n  1. Spawn hook script with timeout from `timeout:` field.\n  2. Capture stdout (envelope-parsed), stderr (line-captured), and\n     exit code.\n  3. If exit code != 0:\n     a. The tool call MUST continue (fail-open) — the hook does NOT\n        block the matched tool's result from reaching the agent.\n     b. The hook's stderr MUST be propagated to the eval transcript\n        (so the human reader sees what went wrong).\n     c. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\",\n        hook:<script-name>, exit_code:<n>, stderr:<truncated>, ...}`\n        row MUST be appended to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`.\n  4. If exit code == 0:\n     normal-path telemetry only (no hook-errors.jsonl row).\n```\n\nThe fixture script (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13) is\n`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` — a deterministic-exit\nshell script that prints a known message to stderr and exits with\na non-zero code. The eval registers it as the PostToolUse Read\nhandler in a test-only hooks.json variant; the inputs trigger a\nRead; the harness fires the failing hook; the harness logs the\nerror and fails open; the assertions pin all three observable\nguarantees.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists post-run | proves the harness opened the error ledger at all |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"hook_error\"` substring | proves the harness emitted a hook_error-typed row |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"` substring | proves the disposition was fail-open (vs. fail-closed) |\n| `stderr` contains `\"failing-post-read.sh\"` | proves the hook's stderr was captured + surfaced to the eval transcript |\n| Process exits cleanly (exit_code == 0) | proves the failing hook did NOT propagate up to the eval-process exit code (the fail-open contract decouples hook exit from process exit) |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E13 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor surfaces\nstale ~/.claude/settings.json schema\"`, `category: doctor` — left\nover from the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was a doctor-surface eval).\nT05.19 replaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Hook stderr error fails open\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E13) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E12; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the harness's hook error-handling path, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the failing fixture is a pure shell script, no MCP, no network |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E12 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by 1 Write + 1 Read + /quit through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | seed `fixture.txt` via Write so the subsequent Read has a target (mirrors E9's fixture-seeding pattern) |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | trigger a Read of `fixture.txt`; this is the call whose PostToolUse hook (the failing fixture) returns non-zero — the surface this eval asserts; carries `expect_tool_call: Read` to pin the matched tool |\n| **inputs[2].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"hook_error\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"' }` |\n| **expects[3]** | `stderr: { contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\" }` |\n| **expects[4]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the failing fixture is a\npure shell script; no MCP server is required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `contains_event(type=..., disposition=...)` proxy\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expect shape names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass. The proxy is **necessary\n  but not sufficient** for the strict semantic: it does not prove\n  the two substrings appeared on the SAME JSONL row (a hypothetical\n  pathological emit pattern that wrote `{type:hook_error,\n  disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and `{type:other_thing,\n  disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would satisfy the\n  proxies but violate the strict semantic). In practice the\n  harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\n  row by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\n  the strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\n  across rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing.\n- **Event-count proxy:** none — the OQ-2 shape doesn't require\n  `event_count == 1`, only `contains_event` (at-least-once), so\n  the substring presence proxy is a complete coverage of the\n  at-least-once aspect.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives, matching mechanism (Expect.file with JSONL\nsubstring), matching deferral footprint (declarative DSL\nexpressibility limitation). The conjunctive `{type, matcher}`\nshape converges with the strict semantic for the same reason\ndescribed above (the harness emits all matcher-pin fields on a\nsingle row by design).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — the failing fixture + hooks.json variant deferral\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires a **test-only hooks.json variant**\nthat registers `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` as the\nPostToolUse Read handler. Three preconditions are needed:\n\n1. **The fixture script `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`\n   must exist on disk.** Today it does not (per the codebase grep:\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory does not exist; no\n   `failing-post-read*` file anywhere in the repo). A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the fixture as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **A hooks.json-variant deployment path must exist.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` field on `evalEntry` and no\n   `inputs[].setup:` callback that could swap in a test-only\n   hooks.json with the failing fixture registered. Two options\n   for landing this:\n   - (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) — a callback that\n     writes a test-only hooks.json to `<home>/.claude/hooks.json`\n     and `<home>/.claude/settings.json` before the PTY session\n     opens;\n   - (b) a new `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field that\n     points to a test-only hooks.json fixture, with the setup\n     wrapper preferring the variant over the production hooks.json\n     when present.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger must be wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is responsible for\n   wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on the\n   non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nUntil all three land, E13's full end-to-end execution path\n(`eval run --suite real --eval E13`) will surface predictable\nfailures: the harness deploys the production hooks.json (no\nfailing fixture is registered), no PostToolUse hook fails, no\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` is written, and the eval ERRORs with\n\"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\". This is the correct\nfailure mode — surfacing each missing precondition incrementally.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\nestablished by T05.07..T05.17. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 are\nmet by the describe / list / round-trip evidence (the manifest body\nis FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped, and resolves through\n`Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring full end-to-end\n`eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on (i) the\nrunner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired.\n\n### Why `expect_tool_call: Read` (not just a free-form prompt)\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\n### Why a seed Write before the Read\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse hook on the Write matcher\nbranch (covered independently by E7) — that co-fire is harmless\nbecause E13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by\n`type:\"hook_error\"` on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit\ntelemetry on `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E13` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17\nevals under suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The failing fixture (once landed per §3 scaffolding gap) is a\n  deterministic-exit shell script — same exit code + same stderr\n  message on every invocation, with no time-of-day or network\n  dependencies.\n- The harness's error-logging path (once wired) is a pure-append to\n  `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` — no shared-state dependencies, no\n  ordering ambiguity (the failing hook fires once per matched Read,\n  the eval triggers exactly one Read, so the ledger contains exactly\n  one error row).\n- The asserted substrings (`\"type\":\"hook_error\"`,\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`, `\"failing-post-read.sh\"`) are\n  invariant across runs (the `ts`, `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`,\n  `stderr` (truncated copy) fields on the JSONL row, once emitted,\n  are not asserted against).\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E13` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The determinism\ncontract is independent of the scaffolding gap — once the gap\ncloses, the body is determined by construction.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` and `inputs[].expect_tool_call: string`\n  (additionalProperties: true under `evalEntry.inputs.items` per\n  `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved\n  at load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (3×`file`, 1×`stderr`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `stderr` primitive kwarg `contains` is supported by\n  `Expect.stderr._build` (`expect.py:556-569`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E13 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 scaffolding gap) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; failing fixture is pure shell |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.19, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E13\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03..T05.17 evidence blocks all block any direct\n`eval run` invocation. That blocker is the responsibility of the\nrunner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency of the CP-P05-T13-T17\ncheckpoint at T05.18). T05.19 authors the manifest body; observable\nverification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/describe-E13.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/list-with-E13.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls into\nthe runner-completion task downstream of T05.19, AND the\nscaffolding-gap closure tasks described in §8.1.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — scaffolding gap + contains_event predicate\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 specifies a four-part contract:\n\n1. **Tool call completes successfully (fail-open).** Landed verbatim\n   via `exit_code.equals(0)` — the PTY-teardown exit code being 0\n   is the necessary downstream observable for \"the failing hook\n   did not propagate up to the eval-process exit code\". (Strict\n   form would also assert the Read tool's specific result reached\n   the agent; deferred to a future per-tool-result primitive.)\n2. **Stderr captured.** Landed verbatim via\n   `stderr.contains(\"failing-post-read.sh\")` — the OQ-2-named\n   `stderr.contains(failing_hook_script_name)` assertion, with\n   the script name literalized from the OQ-2-named fixture path.\n3. **Hook-error ledger row present with `{type:hook_error,\n   disposition:fail_open}` fields.** Landed with the declarative\n   proxy: three `Expect.file` rows on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n   (one for existence, one for each of the two substring pins).\n   Strict `Expect.jsonl.contains_event(type=hook_error,\n   disposition=fail_open)` requires a Python callable filter\n   (expect.py:269-369), not expressible in declarative YAML.\n4. **The harness wires all of the above.** **Deferred** — the\n   harness today does not (a) deploy a hooks.json variant with the\n   failing fixture, (b) emit a structured hook-error ledger on\n   non-zero hook exit, or (c) include a fixture script at\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`. See §3 \"Scaffolding\n   gap\" for the three sub-deferrals.\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.17 (telemetry gaps in\nfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\ndeclaratively, \"twice + digest\" deferrals), T05.19 lands the OQ-2\nbody shape with the best-effort declarative proxies for branches\n(1)-(3) and defers branch (4) to follow-up tasks gated on:\n\n- (a) the failing fixture script landing at\n  `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`;\n- (b) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) OR a new\n  `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field, plus the per-eval\n  setup wrapper honoring the variant deployment;\n- (c) the harness's structured hook-error ledger being wired into\n  the hook-exec path (commands.py / runner.py) to emit\n  `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\", hook:..., exit_code:...,\n  stderr:..., ts:..., session_id:..., tool_call_idx:...}` rows to\n  `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl` on non-zero hook exit;\n- (d) a future schema bump adding declarative\n  `jsonl.contains_event:` primitive shorthand (optional — the\n  two-substring proxy is operationally meaningful as documented\n  in §3 above).\n\n(d) is optional because the two-substring proxy converges with the\nstrict semantic in the absence of pathological emit patterns; (a)-(c)\nare required for ANY end-to-end execution of E13 to pass at all.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: once (a)-(c) land, the body\ncatches every regression in the harness's fail-open discipline —\nmissing ledger file, wrong type field, wrong disposition field,\nswallowed stderr, propagated hook exit code. The strict\n`contains_event` form (d) only adds discrimination against\npathological emit-patterns that split the two pinned fields across\nmultiple JSONL rows, which is itself an emit-pattern bug worth\nsurfacing through other channels.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.19; it predates the\ndeliverable and is structural to the declarative DSL's\nexpressibility envelope PLUS the harness's current error-handling\nmaturity. That's the OQ-2\n18\tcontract verbatim.\n19\t\n20\tTwo of those three operations are not expressible declaratively:\n21\t\n22\t- \"invoke the adapter\" requires a Python callback hook the schema\n23\t  does not define;\n24\t- \"snapshot two states and diff\" requires either a `digest_unchanged`\n25\t  predicate or a callback that stages both calls.\n26\t\n27\tWhat IS expressible declaratively is the **post-first-deploy\n28\tshape**: after the harness's setup wrapper runs the adapter once\n29\t(per NFR-ISO2 / T02.13), the per-eval HOME's settings.json should\n30\tcarry registrations for every hook event in hooks.json. That's the\n31\t\"first deploy\" half of the OQ-2 contract, landed via six\n32\t`Expect.settings_json` exists checks (one per hook event key).\n33\t\n34\tThe \"second deploy + digest unchanged\" half is deferred per the\n35\tsame posture taken in T05.07..T05.16 — land the OQ-2 body\n36\tverbatim with the declarative proxies, document the gap in §8.1,\n37\tdefer the strict form to a follow-up task gated on the YAML\n38\tcallback escape hatch / a future schema extension.\n39\t\n40\t## Why settings.json key-path enumeration (not a single deep check)\n41\t\n42\t`Expect.settings_json` (`expect.py:373-480`) traverses dot-separated\n43\tkey segments through `Mapping` values only — it short-circuits at\n44\tthe first array boundary (`expect.py:423-429`). The merged\n45\tsettings.json has the shape:\n46\t\n47\t```jsonc\n48\t{\n49\t  \"hooks\": {\n50\t    \"SessionStart\": [{ \"hooks\": [...] }, ...],     // array of entries\n51\t    \"PreToolUse\": [{ \"matcher\": \"...\", \"hooks\": [...] }, ...],\n52\t    ...\n53\t  }\n54\t}\n55\t```\n56\t\n57\t`hooks.SessionStart` resolves to the array of entries (Mapping →\n58\tkey → list); `hooks.SessionStart.0` would require array indexing\n59\tthe primitive doesn't support. The deepest expressible check is\n60\t**top-level event presence**: `hooks.<event>` exists.\n61\t\n62\tSix independent checks (one per hook event) collectively prove:\n63\t\n64\t- the merged settings.json was written at all (FR-ISO2 / setup\n65\t  wrapper correctness);\n66\t- every hook event from the source `hooks.json` was merged in (no\n67\t  event silently dropped);\n68\t- the top-level `hooks` key is present and structured as a Mapping.\n69\t\n70\tThis is sufficient discrimination for the \"first deploy is\n71\tcorrect\" regression class. Finer granularity (per-matcher pin,\n72\tper-script command pin, per-timeout pin) requires array traversal\n73\tor a callback predicate — both deferred.\n74\t\n75\t## Why `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (not an empty inputs[])\n76\t\n77\tE12 has no in-session work to do — the install_hooks invocation\n78\tthat produces the asserted shape is performed by the setup wrapper\n79\tbefore the PTY session opens. Strictly speaking, the eval could\n80\tomit `inputs` entirely (the schema allows it) and let the PTY\n81\tharness sit idle.\n82\t\n83\tIn practice the PTY harness needs a clean exit path to satisfy\n84\t`exit_code.equals(0)`. Sibling E3-E11 all use `/quit` as the final\n85\tinput for this reason; an empty inputs[] would cause the harness\n86\tto timeout-kill the session and yield a non-zero exit code. T05.17\n87\tinherits this pattern: a single-element `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]`\n88\tgives the PTY a clean shutdown trigger without doing any\n89\tin-session work.\n90\t\n91\tThe `/quit` also serves a secondary purpose: it advertises to the\n92\treader that \"this eval intentionally doesn't drive Claude through\n93\tany tool calls\" — the body's only behavioral contract is on the\n94\tpost-setup settings.json shape. A reader skimming `inputs[]`\n95\tsees `/quit` immediately and understands the eval is a\n96\tpost-setup assertion suite, not a hook-firing scenario.\n97\t\n98\t## Why `category: hook-lifecycle` (not `installer` or `doctor`)\n99\t\n100\tThe stale E12 placeholder carried `category: doctor` (a leftover\n101\tfrom the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E12 was \"doctor surfaces missing\n102\tclaude binary\"). The OQ-2 resolution reassigned E12 to the\n103\t`install_hooks` adapter, which prompts a category re-think:\n104\t\n105\t- `installer` would be a reasonable choice (the surface is the\n106\t  installer), but no other eval in the suite uses this category\n107\t  and creating a singleton category creates noise in `eval list`\n108\t  filtering.\n109\t- `doctor` is wrong — E12 doesn't exercise any doctor surface.\n110\t- `hook-lifecycle` is the sibling category for E3-E11. E12 covers\n111\t  the deployer half of the hook lifecycle (how hooks get on disk);\n112\t  E3-E11 cover the firing half (how hooks emit events). The\n113\t  category groups them cleanly under one heading for filtering\n114\t  and reporting purposes.\n115\t\n116\tT05.17 picks `hook-lifecycle` for sibling parity and to avoid a\n117\tsingleton `installer` category. A future \"installer\" suite (if\n118\tone is created — TBD per roadmap) would re-shard these.\n119\t\n120\t## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n121\t\n122\t`install_hooks` is pure Python stdlib + filesystem ops (json,\n123\tshutil, os, pathlib). It does no network I/O, calls no MCP\n124\tservers, and depends on no external binaries beyond Python itself.\n125\tPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E12's row lists no capability\n126\ttag.\n127\t\n128\tThe practical implication: E12 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\n129\tmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\n130\tway E12 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n131\tMatches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E11.\n132\t\n133\t## What this body does NOT assert (and why)\n134\t\n135\tThe OQ-2 body row E12 frames three explicit contracts:\n136\t\n137\t1. **Registration presence (first deploy)** — landed verbatim via\n138\t   six `settings_json.exists` checks.\n139\t2. **Registration presence (second deploy)** — deferred (no\n140\t   callback to invoke the adapter twice).\n141\t3. **Digest unchanged (idempotency proof)** — deferred (no\n142\t   `digest_unchanged` predicate; no callback to stage two\n143\t   snapshots).\n144\t\n145\tThe PR #49 regression class is on contract (3) specifically — a\n146\tmalformed merge that duplicates an existing matcher entry. The\n147\tlanded body catches contracts (1) but NOT (3). If the user\n148\tdemands strict PR #49 coverage before the deferred form lands, the\n149\tfollow-up task should:\n150\t\n151\t(a) prioritize the YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) for the\n152\t    schema; OR\n153\t(b) add a declarative `Expect.file.digest_unchanged_after:` /\n154\t    similar primitive backed by a Python implementation that can\n155\t    invoke the adapter (or any callable) between snapshots; AND\n156\t(c) update E12 to use the new escape hatch and add a third\n157\t    expects row pinning the digest.\n158\t\n159\tThe defer-to-follow-up posture is consistent with T05.07..T05.16\n160\t(which defer event_count predicates / per-prompt count\n161\tdiscrimination for similar reasons). All\n  primitives used (3×`file`, 1×`stderr`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `stderr` primitive kwarg `contains` is supported by\n  `Expect.stderr._build` (`expect.py:556-569`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E13 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 scaffolding gap) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; failing fixture is pure shell |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).","numLines":200,"startLine":150,"totalLines":490}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_0147aa5SN1d18Erm4BeroKbs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_011DJq4Yfyx38iLmFL4AYnUv","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0097/notes.md","content":"# D-0097 — Notes / Design Rationale\n\n## Why E13's surface is the harness, not a hook\n\nE3-E11 each pin one row of `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — the\neval drives Claude Code through the PTY, fires the matched hook, and\nreads the freshness ledger to prove the hook script emitted its\nevent. E12 pins the **deployer** (`hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`)\nvia post-setup settings.json shape. E13 is structurally distinct\nfrom all of them: its surface is the **harness's hook-execution\nerror-handling path** — the code that wraps every hook invocation,\ncaptures stderr / exit code, and decides what to do when the hook\nreturns non-zero.\n\nThe body is hook-event-agnostic by design. A failing PostToolUse\nRead hook happens to be the concrete fixture (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4\nrow E13), but the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing\nPreToolUse Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook. The harness\nmust fail-open uniformly regardless of which hook event fired.\n\nThe regression class motivating E13 (per design-spec §11 / OQ-2\nD-0082 §4 notes) is: a hook script's stderr message surfaces to the\nagent **as if the matched tool had failed**. The agent sees the\nhook's error text instead of the tool's result. This is a\ncorrectness/UX bug in the harness's error-propagation path — the\nhook layer is supposed to be invisible to the agent's tool-call\ncontract.\n\nThe natural body shape for this surface is: register a known-failing\nhook, fire the matched tool, and assert all three observable\nguarantees of the fail-open contract:\n\n1. The tool call completes (process exits cleanly).\n2. The failing hook's stderr is captured to the eval transcript\n   (a human reader can see what went wrong, even though the agent\n   does not).\n3. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row\n   is appended to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (auditable post-run).\n\nThat's the OQ-2 contract verbatim. The body assertions pin all three\nguarantees with the best-effort declarative proxies.\n\n## Why two `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring proxies for `{type, disposition}`\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expects entry names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass.\n\nThe proxy is **necessary but not sufficient** for the strict\nsemantic: it does not prove the two substrings appeared on the SAME\nJSONL row. A hypothetical pathological emit pattern that wrote\n`{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and\n`{type:other_thing, disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would\nsatisfy the proxies but violate the strict semantic. In practice\nthe harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\nrow by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\nthe strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\nacross rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing as a separate\nERROR.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives (`Expect.file` with JSONL substring), matching\nmechanism (substring presence on the ledger file), matching\ndeferral footprint (declarative DSL expressibility limitation). The\nconjunctive `{type, matcher}` shape converges with the strict\nsemantic for the same reason described above (the harness emits all\nmatcher-pin fields on a single row by design).\n\n## Why a seed `Write` before the `Read`\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\n\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse Write matcher hook\nindependently (covered by E7) — that co-fire is harmless because\nE13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`\non `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit telemetry on\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`. Co-firing of the production\n`pre_edit_freshness.sh` does NOT emit anything to\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (it exits cleanly).\n\nThe PostToolUse Read hook is the one that's replaced by the failing\nfixture in the test-only hooks.json variant. The Read input\n(`inputs[1]`) is the surface this eval asserts. The Write input\n(`inputs[0]`) is pure scaffolding.\n\n## Why `expect_tool_call: Read` on the second input\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\nThe seed Write input (`inputs[0]`) intentionally omits\n`expect_tool_call`. A reasonable agent might satisfy \"create\nfixture.txt with the line 'content'\" via `Write` OR via `Bash\necho`. Pinning a specific tool would make the seed brittle. The\nmatching tool-call assertion is on the *second* input — the one\nwhose hook is the surface under test.\n\n## Why `inputs: [Write, Read, /quit]` (not a single `/quit`)\n\nE12 uses `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` because its assertions are on\npost-setup state (no in-session tool calls required to materialize\nthe asserted shape). E13 is opposite: the assertions are on\n**post-hook-execution** state (`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists\nbecause a hook fired and failed during a tool call). The eval MUST\nactually fire the failing hook in-session, which requires firing the\nPostToolUse Read matcher, which requires actually invoking Read,\nwhich requires a target file. Hence: Write seed → Read trigger →\n/quit teardown.\n\nThe `/quit` is the same clean-shutdown trigger used by E3-E12 — it\nlets the PTY harness reach EOF and the runner emit the eval-process\nexit code without timing out.\n\n## Why `category: hook-lifecycle` (not `harness` or `error-handling`)\n\nThe stale E13 placeholder carried `category: doctor` (a leftover\nfrom the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was \"doctor surfaces stale\nsettings.json schema\"). The OQ-2 resolution reassigned E13 to the\nhook stderr error-path, which prompts a category re-think:\n\n- `harness` or `error-handling` would be technically accurate (the\n  surface IS the harness's error-handling path), but no other eval\n  in the suite uses these categories and creating singleton\n  categories creates noise in `eval list` filtering.\n- `doctor` is wrong — E13 doesn't exercise any doctor surface.\n- `hook-lifecycle` is the sibling category for E3-E12. E13 covers\n  the error-path half of the hook lifecycle (what happens when a\n  hook returns non-zero); E3-E11 cover the happy-path firing half\n  (how hooks emit events on clean exit); E12 covers the deployer\n  half (how hooks get on disk). The category groups all three under\n  one heading for filtering and reporting purposes.\n\nT05.19 picks `hook-lifecycle` for sibling parity, matching the\nT05.17 / E12 precedent.\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe failing fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) is\na pure shell script. It does no network I/O, calls no MCP servers,\nand depends on no external binaries beyond `printf` + `exit`. The\nharness's hook-execution wrapper is also pure Python stdlib (no MCP\ndependency). Per D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E13's row lists\nno capability tag.\n\nThe practical implication: E13 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only way\nE13 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag). Matches\nsiblings E3-E7 / E9-E12.\n\n## What this body does NOT assert (and why)\n\nThe OQ-2 body row E13 frames three explicit observable contracts +\ntwo implicit derived contracts:\n\n1. **Tool call completes** (exit_code == 0) — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.exit_code(equals=0)`.\n2. **Hook stderr captured to transcript** — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.stderr(contains=\"failing-post-read.sh\")`.\n3. **Structured `{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_open}` row\n   emitted** — landed via the two-substring proxy (see \"Why two\n   `Expect.file(contains=…)`\" above) + a presence check for the\n   ledger file itself.\n4. **(Implicit) Same-row conjunction** — deferred (no\n   `jsonl.contains_event` declarative primitive).\n5. **(Implicit) Event count == 1** (the failing hook fires once per\n   matched Read; the eval triggers exactly one Read; the ledger\n   contains exactly one error row) — NOT asserted. The OQ-2 shape\n   names `contains_event` (at-least-once), not `event_count == 1`,\n   so the substring presence proxy is complete coverage of the\n   at-least-once aspect. If a future regression caused the harness\n   to emit the row N>1 times (e.g., a retry loop that doesn't\n   short-circuit on fail-open), E13 would still pass — the\n   regression would surface elsewhere (e.g., performance regression\n   on hook latency).\n\nThe same-row conjunction (contract 4) is the weakest gap. The\nfollow-up path is the same as T05.17 / E12: a YAML callback escape\nhatch (D-4) OR a future declarative `jsonl.contains_event:`\nprimitive backed by a Python implementation that can express\nconjunctive predicates over JSONL rows.\n\nThe defer-to-follow-up posture is consistent with T05.07..T05.17\n(which defer event_count predicates / per-prompt count\ndiscrimination / digest-unchanged primitives for similar reasons).\nThe deferred branches are documented in §8.1 of the spec; the proxy\nassertions in the landed body are necessary-but-not-sufficient\ncoverage of the OQ-2 contract.\n\n## Scaffolding-gap inheritance (NEW dimension vs. T05.17)\n\nT05.17 (E12) inherited zero scaffolding gaps — the install_hooks\nadapter exists, the setup wrapper invokes it, and the\npost-first-deploy shape is materialized today on every eval run.\nT05.19 (E13) inherits **three scaffolding gaps** that, until closed,\nwill cause `eval run --eval E13` to ERROR rather than PASS:\n\n1. **The fixture script does not exist.**\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` is not in the repo\n   (the `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory itself does not exist).\n   Until it lands, no failing hook can be registered. A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the script as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **No hooks.json-variant deployment path exists.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field and no `inputs[].setup:`\n   callback that could swap in a test-only hooks.json. Two options\n   for closing this gap (documented in spec §3 / §8.1):\n   (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4), or (b) a new\n   `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field with setup-wrapper\n   support.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger is not wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is\n   responsible for wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on\n   the non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nThe acceptance criteria for T05.19 are met by the describe / list /\nround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\nand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the AC requiring full\nend-to-end `eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on\n(i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired. Items (ii)-(iv) are the **NEW** dimension vs. T05.17 —\nthis is the deepest scaffolding-gap inheritance of any post-OQ-2\neval body landed so far.\n\n## Failure-mode discrimination (what an ERROR vs. FAIL means under E13)\n\nOnce the scaffolding gaps close, E13's failure modes are\ndiscriminable:\n\n- **Harness error-path wired but disposition is fail-closed** →\n  `Expect.file(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"')`\n  FAILs → eval status FAIL → signals the harness DID emit the error\n  ledger but chose the wrong disposition (fail-closed). This is a\n  correctness regression on the fail-open contract.\n- **Harness error-path wired but stderr not surfaced** →\n  `Expect.stderr(contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\")` FAILs → eval\n  FAIL → signals the harness emitted the structured ledger row but\n  did NOT propagate the hook's stderr to the eval transcript. This\n  is a UX regression (the human reader can't see what went wrong).\n- **Harness error-path wired but tool exit propagates** →\n  `Expect.exit_code(equals: 0)` FAILs → eval FAIL → signals the\n  harness did NOT decouple hook exit from process exit (fail-closed\n  via process termination). This is the strongest form of the\n  fail-open regression.\n- **Harness error-path NOT wired** → `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` does\n  not exist → all three file-substring expects ERROR (the file\n  doesn't exist, so the `exists:true` check ERRORs before the\n  `contains:` check runs) → eval status ERROR → signals the\n  scaffolding gap (3) is still open.\n- **hooks.json-variant not deployed** → production hooks.json fires\n  the clean-exit `freshness-post-read.sh` instead of the failing\n  fixture → no hook returns non-zero → no `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  is written → all three file-substring expects ERROR → eval status\n  ERROR → indistinguishable from \"harness error-path not wired\"\n  without inspecting the eval transcript. The inspection hint: the\n  eval transcript for the variant-not-deployed case shows clean\n  freshness emissions on `logs/freshness.jsonl`; for the\n  harness-not-wired case shows a failing-fixture stderr line in the\n  raw PTY transcript but no structured ledger row.\n\nSibling E3-E12 evals depend on the setup wrapper and the production\nhooks.json firing correctly; E13 depends additionally on (ii) and\n(iii) above. The scaffolding-gap framing in §8.1 of the spec\ndocuments this explicitly to set expectations for the runner-\ncompletion task downstream.\n\n## Inheritance from sibling deferral pattern\n\nThe deferral posture in §8.1 follows the same template established\nby T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1), T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1), T05.09 (D-0089\n§8.1), T05.10/T05.11/T05.13 (D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), T05.14\n(D-0093 §8.1), T05.15 (D-0094 §3 footnote), T05.16 (D-0095 embedded\nin real.yaml E11 comment block), and T05.17 (D-0096 §8.1):\n\n| Task | Deferred construct | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| T05.07..T05.14 | freshness-ledger emit (script telemetry gap) | scripts write to bare integer counters, not OQ-2-contract JSONL |\n| T05.15 | `jsonl.event_count(...) >= 1` | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.16 | `event_count(start) == event_count(stop)` symmetry | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.17 | `install_hooks` second invocation + digest unchanged | needs YAML callback escape hatch + digest primitive |\n| **T05.19 (this)** | `jsonl.contains_event(type=…, disposition=…)` same-row conjunction + hooks.json-variant deployment + harness structured-error-ledger wiring | needs YAML callback escape hatch (or `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field) + new declarative primitive (or callback) + harness implementation |\n\nEach deferral lands the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\ndeclarative proxy, documents the gap explicitly, and gates the\nstrict form on a future schema/feature/implementation landing.\nT05.19 inherits this pattern with the deepest A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row\n   is appended to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (auditable post-run).\n\nThat's the OQ-2 contract verbatim. The body assertions pin all three\nguarantees with the best-effort declarative proxies.\n\n## Why two `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring proxies for `{type, disposition}`\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expects entry names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass.\n\nThe proxy is **necessary but not sufficient** for the strict\nsemantic: it does not prove the two substrings appeared on the SAME\nJSONL row. A hypothetical pathological emit pattern that wrote\n`{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and\n`{type:other_thing, disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would\nsatisfy the proxies but violate the strict semantic. In practice\nthe harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\nrow by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\nthe strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\nacross rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing as a separate\nERROR.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives (`Expect.file` with JSONL substring), matching\nmechanism (substring presence on the ledger file), matching\ndeferral footprint (declarative DSL expressibility limitation). The\nconjunctive `{type, matcher}` shape converges with the strict\nsemantic for the same reason described above (the harness emits all\nmatcher-pin fields on a single row by design).\n\n## Why a seed `Write` before the `Read`\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\n\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse Write matcher hook\nindependently (covered by E7) — that co-fire is harmless because\nE13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`\non `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit telemetry on\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`. Co-firing of the production\n`pre_edit_freshness.sh` does NOT emit anything to\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (it exits cleanly).\n\nThe PostToolUse Read hook is the one that's replaced by the failing\nfixture in the test-only hooks.json variant. The Read input\n(`inputs[1]`) is the surface this eval asserts. The Write input\n(`inputs[0]`) is pure scaffolding.\n\n## Why `expect_tool_call: Read` on the second input\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\nThe seed Write input (`inputs[0]`) intentionally omits\n`expect_tool_call`. A reasonable agent might satisfy \"create\nfixture.txt with the line 'content'\" via `Write` OR via `Bash\necho`. Pinning a specific tool would make the seed brittle. The\nmatching tool-call assertion is on the *second* input — the one\nwhose hook is the surface under test.\n\n## Why `inputs: [Write, Read, /quit]` (not a single `/quit`)\n\nE12 uses `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` because its assertions are on\npost-setup state (no in-session tool calls required to materialize\nthe asserted shape). E13 is opposite: the assertions are on\n**post-hook-execution** state (`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists\nbecause a hook fired and failed during a tool call). The eval MUST\nactually fire the failing hook in-session, which requires firing the\nPostToolUse Read matcher, which requires actually invoking Read,\nwhich requires a target file. Hence: Write seed → Read trigger →\n/quit teardown.\n\nThe `/quit` is the same clean-shutdown trigger used by E3-E12 — it\nlets the PTY harness reach EOF and the runner emit the eval-process\nexit code without timing out.\n\n## Why `category: hook-lifecycle` (not `harness` or `error-handling`)\n\nThe stale E13 placeholder carried `category: doctor` (a leftover\nfrom the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was \"doctor surfaces stale\nsettings.json schema\"). The OQ-2 resolution reassigned E13 to the\nhook stderr error-path, which prompts a category re-think:\n\n- `harness` or `error-handling` would be technically accurate (the\n  surface IS the harness's error-handling path), but no other eval\n  in the suite uses these categories and creating singleton\n  categories creates noise in `eval list` filtering.\n- `doctor` is wrong — E13 doesn't exercise any doctor surface.\n- `hook-lifecycle` is the sibling category for E3-E12. E13 covers\n  the error-path half of the hook lifecycle (what happens when a\n  hook returns non-zero); E3-E11 cover the happy-path firing half\n  (how hooks emit events on clean exit); E12 covers the deployer\n  half (how hooks get on disk). The category groups all three under\n  one heading for filtering and reporting purposes.\n\nT05.19 picks `hook-lifecycle` for sibling parity, matching the\nT05.17 / E12 precedent.\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe failing fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) is\na pure shell script. It does no network I/O, calls no MCP servers,\nand depends on no external binaries beyond `printf` + `exit`. The\nharness's hook-execution wrapper is also pure Python stdlib (no MCP\ndependency). Per D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E13's row lists\nno capability tag.\n\nThe practical implication: E13 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only way\nE13 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag). Matches\nsiblings E3-E7 / E9-E12.\n\n## What this body does NOT assert (and why)\n\nThe OQ-2 body row E13 frames three explicit observable contracts +\ntwo implicit derived contracts:\n\n1. **Tool call completes** (exit_code == 0) — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.exit_code(equals=0)`.\n2. **Hook stderr captured to transcript** — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.stderr(contains=\"failing-post-read.sh\")`.\n3. **Structured `{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_open}` row\n   emitted** — landed via the two-substring proxy (see \"Why two\n   `Expect.file(contains=…)`\" above) + a presence check for the\n   ledger file itself.\n4. **(Implicit) Same-row conjunction** — deferred (no\n   `jsonl.contains_event` declarative primitive).\n5. **(Implicit) Event count == 1** (the failing hook fires once per\n   matched Read; the eval triggers exactly one Read; the ledger\n   contains exactly one error row) — NOT asserted. The OQ-2 shape\n   names `contains_event` (at-least-once), not `event_count == 1`,\n   so the substring presence proxy is complete coverage of the\n   at-least-once aspect. If a future regression caused the harness\n   to emit the row N>1 times (e.g., a retry loop that doesn't\n   short-circuit on fail-open), E13 would still pass — the\n   regression would surface elsewhere (e.g., performance regression\n   on hook latency).\n\nThe same-row conjunction (contract 4) is the weakest gap. The\nfollow-up path is the same as T05.17 / E12: a YAML callback escape\nhatch (D-4) OR a future declarative `jsonl.contains_event:`\nprimitive backed by a Python implementation that can express\nconjunctive predicates over JSONL rows.\n\nThe defer-to-follow-up posture is consistent with T05.07..T05.17\n(which defer event_count predicates / per-prompt count\ndiscrimination / digest-unchanged primitives for similar reasons).\nThe deferred branches are documented in §8.1 of the spec; the proxy\nassertions in the landed body are necessary-but-not-sufficient\ncoverage of the OQ-2 contract.\n\n## Scaffolding-gap inheritance (NEW dimension vs. T05.17)\n\nT05.17 (E12) inherited zero scaffolding gaps — the install_hooks\nadapter exists, the setup wrapper invokes it, and the\npost-first-deploy shape is materialized today on every eval run.\nT05.19 (E13) inherits **three scaffolding gaps** that, until closed,\nwill cause `eval run --eval E13` to ERROR rather than PASS:\n\n1. **The fixture script does not exist.**\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` is not in the repo\n   (the `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory itself does not exist).\n   Until it lands, no failing hook can be registered. A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the script as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **No hooks.json-variant deployment path exists.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field and no `inputs[].setup:`\n   callback that could swap in a test-only hooks.json. Two options\n   for closing this gap (documented in spec §3 / §8.1):\n   (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4), or (b) a new\n   `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field with setup-wrapper\n   support.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger is not wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is\n   responsible for wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on\n   the non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nThe acceptance criteria for T05.19 are met by the describe / list /\nround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\nand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the AC requiring full\nend-to-end `eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on\n(i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired. Items (ii)-(iv) are the **NEW** dimension vs. T05.17 —\nthis is the deepest scaffolding-gap inheritance of any post-OQ-2\neval body landed so far.\n\n## Failure-mode discrimination (what an ERROR vs. FAIL means under E13)\n\nOnce the scaffolding gaps close, E13's failure modes are\ndiscriminable:\n\n- **Harness error-path wired but disposition is fail-closed** →\n  `Expect.file(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"')`\n  FAILs → eval status FAIL → signals the harness DID emit the error\n  ledger but chose the wrong disposition (fail-closed). This is a\n  correctness regression on the fail-open contract.\n- **Harness error-path wired but stderr not surfaced** →\n  `Expect.stderr(contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\")` FAILs → eval\n  FAIL → signals the harness emitted the structured ledger row but\n  did NOT propagate the hook's stderr to the eval transcript. This\n  is a UX regression (the human reader can't see what went wrong).\n- **Harness error-path wired but tool exit propagates** →\n  `Expect.exit_code(equals: 0)` FAILs → eval FAIL → signals the\n  harness did NOT decouple hook exit from process exit (fail-closed\n  via process termination). This is the strongest form of the\n  fail-open regression.\n- **Harness error-path NOT wired** → `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` does\n  not exist → all three file-substring expects ERROR (the file\n  doesn't exist, so the `exists:true` check ERRORs before the\n  `contains:` check runs) → eval status ERROR → signals the\n  scaffolding gap (3) is still open.\n- **hooks.json-variant not deployed** → production hooks.json fires\n  the clean-exit `freshness-post-read.sh` instead of the failing\n  fixture → no hook returns non-zero → no `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  is written → all three file-substring expects ERROR → eval status\n  ERROR → indistinguishable from \"harness error-path not wired\"\n  without inspecting the eval transcript. The inspection hint: the\n  eval transcript for the variant-not-deployed case shows clean\n  freshness emissions on `logs/freshness.jsonl`; for the\n  harness-not-wired case shows a failing-fixture stderr line in the\n  raw PTY transcript but no structured ledger row.\n\nSibling E3-E12 evals depend on the setup wrapper and the production\nhooks.json firing correctly; E13 depends additionally on (ii) and\n(iii) above. The scaffolding-gap framing in §8.1 of the spec\ndocuments this explicitly to set expectations for the runner-\ncompletion task downstream.\n\n## Inheritance from sibling deferral pattern\n\nThe deferral posture in §8.1 follows the same template established\nby T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1), T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1), T05.09 (D-0089\n§8.1), T05.10/T05.11/T05.13 (D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), T05.14\n(D-0093 §8.1), T05.15 (D-0094 §3 footnote), T05.16 (D-0095 embedded\nin real.yaml E11 comment block), and T05.17 (D-0096 §8.1):\n\n| Task | Deferred construct | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| T05.07..T05.14 | freshness-ledger emit (script telemetry gap) | scripts write to bare integer counters, not OQ-2-contract JSONL |\n| T05.15 | `jsonl.event_count(...) >= 1` | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.16 | `event_count(start) == event_count(stop)` symmetry | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.17 | `install_hooks` second invocation + digest unchanged | needs YAML callback escape hatch + digest primitive |\n| **T05.19 (this)** | `jsonl.contains_event(type=…, disposition=…)` same-row conjunction + hooks.json-variant deployment + harness structured-error-ledger wiring | needs YAML callback escape hatch (or `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field) + new declarative primitive (or callback) + harness implementation |\n\nEach deferral lands the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\ndeclarative proxy, documents the gap explicitly, and gates the\nstrict form on a future schema/feature/implementation landing.\nT05.19 inherits this pattern with the deepest scaffolding-gap stack\n(three preconditions, vs. T05.17's zero) — but the manifest body\nitself is FR-SCH2-valid and resolves correctly through\n`Expect.from_mapping`, satisfying the per-task acceptance criteria\nfor the body-authoring task.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
